```json
  "summary": "A planning-time routing layer: every task gets a model tier + risk profile from a 5-axis rubric. Parked until ready to pull into a cycle.",
  "description": "> **Status: parked.** Shaped and ready, not yet in a cycle. Pull into cycle planning when ready to build. The issue breakdown below is captured up front so the work is thorough on day one.\n\n**Goal:** For Anton and any agent breaking work into tasks in Shaper, assign every task a model tier and a risk profile at breakdown time without prompting — so model choice and review attention are deliberate and reproducible rather than ad-hoc.\n\n**Key results:**\n\n**KR1 (commit)** — `references/task-sizing.md` exists with all required sections, and `planning-and-task-breakdown` cites it (Step 5 + References) *(foundation)*\n- baseline: no file; the planning skill sizes only by verification granularity\n- target: file exists and passes the reference-pattern checklist (type:reference frontmatter, populated cited_by, 5-axis rubric, reversibility-gated rule, worked example); the planning skill cites it\n- measured over: cycle close\n- how we'll know: `test -f references/task-sizing.md && grep -q task-sizing skills/planning-and-task-breakdown/SKILL.md` plus a section-count check\n\n*Layer 1: discipline · Layer 2: artefact-exists + sections-complete*\n\n**KR2 (commit)** — after the layer lands, ≥90% of task lists filed under `docs/tasks/` carry a model-tier and risk annotation per the reference *(bet)*\n- baseline: 0% — no task list carries the annotation today\n- target: ≥90% of filed task lists in the window carry the annotation line\n- measured over: next 10 task lists (rolling) / next 4 cycles\n- how we'll know: `grep -L 'Model:' docs/tasks/*.md` returns nothing — every filed list carries it; count cached per cycle\n\n*Layer 1: outcome · Layer 2: self-trial protocol*\n\n**KR3 (commit)** — the routing is reproducible and discriminating: across the worked example and 5 held-out tasks, two independent applications of the rubric agree on (tier, review-flag), and the sample spans ≥2 distinct tiers *(brake)*\n- baseline: model choice is non-reproducible — a gut call, no rubric\n- target: the two applications agree on (tier, flag) for the worked example and ≥4 of 5 held-out tasks; ≥2 distinct tiers appear; each agreed assignment is defensible against the rubric\n- measured over: cycle close\n- how we'll know: re-run the rubric twice on the worked example and 5 held-out tasks, then diff the (tier, flag) output (automated half); defensibility is the manual half — one line per task (carve-out: defensibility is irreducibly qualitative)\n\n*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*\n\n*Dimensions: discipline / outcome / correctness — all distinct per rule 5b.*\n\n**Affected repos:** agent-skills-shaper\n\n**Appetite:** ~7 issues\n\n**Kill condition:** across 2 consecutive cycles, the rubric neither changes nor defensibly justifies the model you would have picked anyway (no decision value); or the annotation is routinely skipped (KR2 < 50%) — ceremony, not behaviour change.\n\n**Project type:** 1 — Methodology skill pack\n\n---\n\n## Design substance (research-grounded — see sources)\n\n- **Routing, not cascading.** This is planning-time pre-classification (one model picked per task before it runs), not runtime escalation. Cascading is a named, out-of-scope future layer.\n- **Anthropic's decision triad: capabilities / speed / cost**, with a static orchestrator(Opus) / worker(Sonnet) assignment pattern.\n- **5 active axes** (scored Low/Med/High): reasoning complexity, specification completeness, hallucination sensitivity, stakes/reversibility, orchestration role.\n- **3 set-aside dimensions** (named, near-constant for slice-bounded async dev tasks): cost/volume, latency, context size.\n- **Derived outputs:** model tier + review-attention flag both fall out of the axes. Operator review-load is an *output*, not an input.\n- **Default rule — reversibility-gated:** one-way-door / high-stakes tasks default *up* (start Frontier, justify any downgrade); reversible / mechanical tasks default *down* (start cheap, escalate on a capability gap). Grounded in eng-universal P3.\n- **Framing:** risk-weighted routing, *not* effort estimation — a second lens on the existing verification-granularity Size check (reconciles eng-agentic P8).\n- **Model tiers:** Fast = Haiku 4.5 · Balanced = Sonnet 4.6 · Frontier = Opus 4.7 (one mapping line, so a model bump is a one-line edit).\n- **No new principle needed** — an application of eng-universal P3 and eng-agentic P2/P8.\n\n## Issue breakdown (~7)\n\nIssue 1 is the reference-only first cut; 2–3 make it live; 4–7 are the comprehensive reach.\n\n| # | Issue | Done when | Stage |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Write `references/task-sizing.md` (5-axis rubric, tiers, reversibility-gated rule, derived outputs, annotation format, worked example) | file exists + passes the reference-pattern checklist | MVP |\n| 2 | Thread into `planning-and-task-breakdown` Step 5 + References (two-lens framing) | a sample run annotates every task | MVP |\n| 3 | Add `model` + `risk` fields to the task-list template | template carries the fields; a sample list populates them | MVP |\n| 4 | Thread the model tier into `initiative-shape` (initiative-level tier note) | template references task-sizing; cited_by updated | later |\n| 5 | Thread a tier/risk column into `roadmap-shape` | roadmap template carries the column | later |\n| 6 | Enforcement hook (per `docs/hook-anatomy.md`) flagging un-annotated tasks | deterministic pass/fail on a fixture with/without the annotation | later |\n| 7 | Sub-agent `model:` convention — update `docs/sub-agent-anatomy.md`; map worker personas to Fast/Balanced | anatomy doc states the convention; ≥1 persona carries a tier | later |\n\n## Sources\n\n- Anthropic — Models overview: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/overview\n- Anthropic — Choosing the right model: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/docs/about-claude/models/choosing-a-model\n- Anthropic — Multi-agent research system (orchestrator/worker): https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/multi-agent-research-system\n- Augment — AI model routing guide: https://www.augmentcode.com/guides/ai-model-routing-guide\n- TianPan — LLM routing vs model cascades: https://tianpan.co/blog/2025-11-03-llm-routing-model-cascades"
}
```

## What was created

**[Per-task model routing — deliberate, not ad-hoc](https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/per-ta[REDACTED_SK])** — Linear project, **Planned / parked** (not in any cycle), team Personal (`ABA`).

It carries the full six-field shape (Goal · 3 KRs with grader-backed sub-fields and foundation/bet/brake roles · affected repos · ~7-issue appetite · kill condition · Type 1), plus the research-grounded design substance and the 7-issue breakdown, so it's thorough the day you pull it in — not a stub.

## How we got here

1. **Brainstormed and refined** the idea via `idea-refine`'s divergent→convergent frame.
2. **Research-grounded the criteria** (you flagged your three as made-up) — corrected against Anthropic's docs + routing literature: kept hallucination-sensitivity, split "domain complexity" into three real axes, found review-load is a *derived output* not an input, and added the missing constraints half. Landed on **5 active axes + reversibility-gated routing**.
3. **Reframed** from an implementation plan to a plan-to-shape-in-Linear (your review comment).
4. **Reshaped the KRs** to the Type 1 methodology rubric (invocation + decision-quality, not artefact volume).
5. **Polished** the prose through `/writing-clearly-and-concisely`.
6. **Created it parked.**

I also saved two working-style notes to memory: shape-and-park new features, and research-ground proposed criteria.

## Your next move, when ready

The 7 issues live in the description, not as Linear issues yet — per your `linear-workflow.md`, issues get created at cycle planning. When you want to start, pull it into a cycle and I'll create the issues (issue 1 = write `references/task-sizing.md`). Want me to create those 7 issues now instead, or leave them in the description until then?

```
## Approved Plan (edited by user):
# Plan: apply the task-sizing rubric to the 7 routing-layer issues and label them in Linear

## Context

The parked initiative *"Per-task model routing — deliberate, not ad-hoc"* (project
`8b7d20a8…`) and its 7 issues (ABA-274 … ABA-280) already exist in Backlog. Anton now
wants to **dogfood the rubric we designed** — apply it to assign a model tier to each of
those 7 issues, and record the assignment as a Linear **label**.

This is a nice validation: it exercises the very rubric ABA-274 will formalise, and it
directly tests the initiative's KR3 ("the routing is reproducible *and discriminating* —
≥2 distinct tiers, not all-Frontier"). The rubric file doesn't exist yet, so I'm applying
the **designed** rubric from our brainstorm, held in the plan history below.

## The rubric (as designed)

Five active axes, each scored **Low / Med / High**:

1. **Reasoning complexity** — how much conceptual design / synthesis the task demands.
2. **Spec completeness** — how fully the task is specified vs. needing design judgment.
3. **Hallucination sensitivity** — exposure to fabricable facts (APIs, version-specific
   model names, source citations). High → also triggers `source-driven-development`.
4. **Stakes / reversibility** — blast radius + cost-of-reversal (universal P3, one-way
   vs two-way door).
5. **Orchestration role** — orchestrator vs worker.

**Tiers:** `Fast = Haiku 4.5 · Balanced = Sonnet 4.6 · Frontier = Opus 4.7`.

**Reversibility-gated default rule:** one-way-door / high-stakes tasks default **up**
(start Frontier, justify any downgrade); reversible / mechanical tasks default **down**
(start cheap, escalate on a capability gap).

**Derived outputs:** model tier **and** a review-attention flag — both fall out of the
same axes (high stakes or high hallucination → flag the artefact for a careful review pass).

## Scoring — the 7 issues

Axes: Reason / Spec / Halluc / Stakes / Role. Tier is the reversibility-gated read of the row.

| Issue | Reason | Spec | Halluc | Stakes | Role | → Tier | Review flag |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ABA-274** Write the rubric reference | High | Med | **High** | **High** (keystone; 6 issues depend on its vocabulary) | worker (keystone) | **Frontier** | **High** |
| **ABA-275** Thread into planning Step 5 GATE | Med | Med | Med | Med (edits a GATE, but reversible prose) | worker | **Balanced** | Med |
| **ABA-276** Add fields to task-list template | Low | High | Low | Low (small, reversible) | worker | **Fast** | Low |
| **ABA-277** Thread tier into initiative-shape | Med | Med | Med | Med (high-traffic skill, reversible) | worker | **Balanced** | Med |
| **ABA-278** Tier/risk column in roadmap-shape | Low | High | Low | Low (contained column add) | worker | **Fast** | Low |
| **ABA-279** Enforcement hook | Med–High (deterministic criteria design) | Med | Med | Med (false-positive friction = blast radius) | worker | **Balanced** | High |
| **ABA-280** Sub-agent `model:` convention | Med | Med | Med (version-specific tier names) | Med (authoritative anatomy doc) | worker | **Balanced** | Med |

**Distribution: 1 Frontier · 4 Balanced · 2 Fast — three distinct tiers, not all-Frontier.**
The rubric discriminates, satisfying the spirit of KR3.

Notable calls:
- **ABA-274 up to Frontier** — only true one-way door: it's the keystone the other six cite,
  it synthesises the research, and it carries the version-specific model names (hallucination-
  High). Default-up rule applies; a downgrade would need justification.
- **ABA-276 / ABA-278 down to Fast** — mechanical, fully-specified, reversible template/column
  edits with no fabricable-fact exposure. Default-down rule applies.
- **ABA-279 stays Balanced, not Fast** — designing *deterministic* hook pass/fail criteria is
  real reasoning, and a hook that false-positives has friction blast radius (review flag High).
  If it later grows executable matching logic, escalate to Frontier.

## Linear label mapping (reuse existing labels — do not create new ones)

The workspace already has `model:opus`, `model:sonnet`, `model:haiku`. Map tier → label:

- Frontier → `model:opus` → **ABA-274**
- Balanced → `model:sonnet` → **ABA-275, ABA-277, ABA-279, ABA-280**
- Fast → `model:haiku` → **ABA-276, ABA-278**

## Execution

For each issue, call `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` with `labels` set to **both** the
existing `repo:agent-skills-shaper` label **and** the mapped `model:*` label (the `labels`
field replaces the set, so the repo label must be re-passed or it's dropped). 7 calls total.

The **review-attention flag** is a second rubric output. Anton asked only for the model
label, so I'll apply just the model labels by default. If he wants the flag recorded too,
the cheapest route is a `review:high` label on ABA-274 and ABA-279 — I'll offer this, not
assume it.

## Verification

After labeling, re-list the project issues (`list_issues` on project `8b7d20a8…`) and
confirm each carries exactly `[repo:agent-skills-shaper, model:<tier>]` with the mapping
above — 1 opus / 4 sonnet / 2 haiku.

```

| **ABA-282** walk-script + conversion proof + A-vs-B decision `[GATE]` | High | Med | **High** (writes real code; must match Linear's milestone/issue/sub-issue schema — external data model) | **High** (the formal GATE + a one-way-door architecture decision) | worker (gatekeeper) | **Frontier** `model:opus` | **High** |

**Both land Frontier / review:high — and here that's the honest read, not a failure to discriminate.** These two issues *are* the riskiest-assumption spike (eng-universal P4 / Rule B2): by construction the highest-stakes, highest-reasoning work in the project. Routing the kill gate to a cheap model is the dangerous error — a weaker model produces a plausible-looking file-set or manifest that hides whether the premise actually holds, giving false confidence at exactly the proceed/kill decision. With n=2 and both being halves of one gate, two-of-two Frontier is correct.

Two honest notes:
- **ABA-282 is unambiguous** — it writes an executable script (`bin/walk-delivery-plan`), it must conform to Linear's real schema (hallucination-High → also triggers `source-driven-development`), and it carries a one-way-door A-vs-B architecture decision. Nothing argues for a downgrade.
- **ABA-281 is the marginal one.** Its hand-production of markdown is the most "authoring-flavored" task here, so if you read it as mechanical extraction from an existing example, Balanced (`model:sonnet`) is defensible. I kept it Frontier because its actual job is judging whether the standalone-markdown premise survives — the kill-condition test — and stakes-High defaults up.

I scored only, as asked — these are in a different project so I didn't touch the labels. Want me to apply them? `model:opus` and `review:high` already exist, so it's two `save_issue` calls: both issues get `model:opus + review:high`. Or, if you read ABA-281 as the mechanical case, I'll set it to `model:sonnet` instead.

Right — to be precise about what I meant, and what I did and didn't do.

**What the claim means.** In the rubric as we designed it, the hallucination axis isn't only an input to the model-tier decision — when it scores **High**, one of the rubric's *derived outputs* is a **companion-skill trigger**: the task should be **built using `source-driven-development`**. That's the agent-skills workflow skill whose whole job is "verify against official docs before implementing" — it's the operational form of eng-agentic P2 (*hallucination is the default; sources are the brake*). So yes: I was saying ABA-282 should be **executed with that skill engaged** — when it writes `bin/walk-delivery-plan`, it must verify Linear's actual milestone/issue/sub-issue schema (field names, hierarchy, what a "sub-issue" really is) against the real data model rather than asserting it from memory, because fabricating that schema is the exact failure mode there.

**What I actually recorded, though — nothing of the sort.** I only applied two labels to ABA-282: `model:opus` and `review:high`. The `source-driven-development` trigger currently lives **only in my explanation**, not on the issue. So it's a claim about what the rubric *would* prescribe, not something captured anywhere an implementer would see it.

That's an honest gap. The rubric produces three derived outputs — model tier, review flag, **and** companion-skill triggers — but I've only made the first two visible in Linear. If you want the third one captured too, the options are:

- a `skill:source-driven-development` label on ABA-282 (parallel to how `review:high` records the review-pass trigger), or
- a one-line note in the issue description, or
- leave it as design intent that ABA-274 will formalise, and not annotate it now.

Which do you want? And note this is itself useful signal for ABA-274: if companion-skill triggers are a real rubric output, they need a representation in Linear, not just prose — same lesson the `review:high` label taught.
